市場焦點
聯準會9月
升息一碼
• 聯邦基準利率升至 3.75%~4.00%，經濟預測部分上調2026-2028年GDP成長率、失業率皆下
調，通膨降溫進程仍相對緩慢 ，點陣圖顯示今年還有升息一碼的機會 ，這次升息對股票而言 ，
聯準會沒有準備以大幅犧牲經濟與就業換取通膨下降 ，降低了硬著陸風險；對債券而言 ，短
端仍須消化更高的政策利率，但長端反而可能受惠於抗通膨可信度改善。
AI模型
開發放緩
• Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度。不過，前沿模型開發速度放
緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。晶片、算力、資料中心及能源等AI基礎建設仍處於供
不應求格局 ，短期若因市場情緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔 ，較可能反映估值與持倉修正 ，

而非AI需求基本面反轉 。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化 ，且全球總體經濟及地緣政治
風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。
台灣央行
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融
通利率分別維持2%、2.375%和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制 ，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最
高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通膨率 ，GDP上修至11.48%、CPI
及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16。(4)央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表
現，不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。

資料來源：Fed，華爾街見聞，2026.9.17
貨幣決議與9月、7月聲明稿差異，不再強調能源衝擊 全員支持升息
• 聯準會9月升息一碼，聯邦基準利率在3.75%~4.00%。
聲明稿重點差異
紅色字體為9月的新增部分，括號內藍色字體為刪除的7月聲明措辭
FOMC以12票贊成升息一碼 批准發布以下聲明 ：委員會決定 ，將聯邦基金利率目標 區間上調至 3.75%至4%維持在
3.50%至3.75%，以支持聯準會的雙重使命。委員會延續其維持銀行體系充足準備金的政策。
儘管中東衝突在一定程度上帶來了高水準的不確定性 ，美國經濟活動持續以穩健步調擴張 。儘管地緣政治情勢 等因素
使經濟前景仍籠 罩高度不確定性，但國內支出保持韌性，生產力成長強勁，企業資本投資也相當強勁穩健。新增就業

人數大致跟上勞動力成長速度，失業率也變化不大，顯示就業市場目前仍大致維持穩定。
相較於委員會2%的目標，通貨膨脹率依然偏高 ，這在一定程度上反映了部分特定部門 (包括能源)因供給衝擊所導致的
價格上漲。今日的政策行動，將有助於讓通膨更及時地回到2%目標，委員會將實現價格穩定。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
1
通膨「過高且持續太久」 當
前重心偏向物價穩定
通膨數據未改善，通膨過高且持續太久，聯準會當前的政策重心偏向雙重使命中的物價穩定 。透過政策
行動表明，聯準會對抑制通膨是認真的。
2
經濟、就業與地緣政治促成
一致升息
近幾週公布的數據證實美國經濟正在走強 ，基礎經濟成長率高於先前判斷；其次 ，勞動市場依然穩健；
第三，通膨持續高於目標，地緣政治局勢也改變經濟展望 。華許表示，這三項因素共同促成當天「堅定
且一致」的決定。
3
點狀圖暗示今年再升 1碼 但
不是政策承諾
最新經濟預測顯示，多數官員認為今年仍有進一步升息空間。12 名官員預期年底前再升息 1 碼，另有 4
人預期還會升息 2 碼，僅 2 人主張利率維持在目前水準。不過，點狀圖反映的是官員對適當利率路徑的

個別預測，並非正式的承諾。
4
拒絕提供前瞻指引 不預判下
次會議結果
面對下一次升息可能落在 10 月或 12 月的追問，華許表示「我不給出前瞻指引。」
他強調，先前公開表達的是對貨幣政策紀律的承諾 ，而不是對特定利率決策的承諾 。聯準會不會預判未
來任何會議結果，後續行動仍將依據最新經濟數據及展望作出決定。
5
升息非市場逼迫 迴避川普施
壓問題
面對此次升息是否由債市波動及市場壓力所推動的提問，華許予以否認。
他表示是根據就業走勢、經濟強度及整體情勢作出決定 ，市場價格可作為觀察依據，但不能決定聯準會
的政策。
資料來源：Fed，華爾街見聞，2026.9.17

市場反應與投資建議
9/16
市場反應
• 市場將華許記者會解讀為偏鷹，那斯達克指數、標普
500指數、道瓊工業指數皆下跌。
• 10 年 期 美 債 殖 利 率 在 利 率 決 議 公 布 後 一 度 降 至
4.9323%，隨後回升至接近 4.99%，在關鍵的5%關
口附近波動。
• 2年期殖利率則由盤中低點4.5975%升至4.7124%，
反映投資人重新提高對後續升息的預期。
• 黃金跌破4,260美元。
• 利率市場顯示 ，交易員加大 聯準會在2026 年底前進
一步升息的押注，12月升息機率升至約50%。
投資建議
• 股市：AI長期趨勢不變，但需留意利率對評價面的影
響，逢拉回就分批買進，相對看好美、台、韓相關供
應鏈
• 債市：預期美債利率 維持高位震盪格局 ，目前債市

配置仍以鎖利為主，且聚焦中短天期。
資料來源：芝商所，2026.9.17

台灣利率連10凍 明年經濟展望佳
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融通利率分別維持 2%、2.375%
和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通
膨率，GDP上修至11.48%(六月預估9.45 %)、CPI及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16%(六月預估1.91%及1.90%)。
• 觀點：全球AI資本支出維持上調的趨勢，台股供應鏈訂單直接受惠，央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表現，
不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。
• 台股短線展望：上週隨著聯準會利率決議公布後，台股量能回升，顯示短線消息面或已利空出盡。權值股台積電領軍台灣加

權指數站上月線，技術指標偏正向。投資佈局方面，由於地緣政治、通膨及貿易關稅風險仍存在不確定性，建議AI概念持股
核心布局行業龍頭企業，留意中小型成長股較易受到外部因素而股價大幅波動。
資料來源：中央銀行，2026.9.17。f代表央行預測數。

美國零售強勁回升擴大內需支撐，惟價格壓力與升息提高市場波動
• 零售全面回升，消費動能展現廣度：美國8月零售銷售月增1.2%，創3月以來最大增幅，優於市場預期，並扭轉7月修正
後月減0.5%；非實體店面銷售月增 2.6%、電子及家電增 1.6%，餐飲服務亦增 1.2%，且扣除汽車與汽油後仍增 1.2%，
控制組更增1.4%，顯示消費回升並非僅由汽車與燃料支出帶動，第三季內需仍具支撐。
• 名目消費偏強，通膨與升息增添需求壓力： 不過8月零售銷售年增6.0%屬名目增幅，部分反映物價上升因素；同期 CPI
年增3.4%，汽油價格月增3.9%，能源成本上升可能壓縮家庭實質購買力 ，加上Fed於9月決議升息1碼，後續利率政策
仍將視通膨與經濟數據調整，使市場焦點由消費回升轉向評估高利率與高物價對後續需求動能的影響。

• 後市觀察：短期零售回升仍有助支撐美國景氣，但若能源成本持續走高，加上升息後借貸成本上升，消費動能能否維持
將成為重要觀察點；投資配置可偏向美國投資級短中天期債券 ，兼顧收益與利率風險，股票則可聚焦獲利穩定、具定價
能力的大型優質企業，降低需求放緩與資金成本升高的影響。
近三月美國零售銷售月率變化(%)

科技企業自由現金流可望由目前約2,450億增至2028年的6,330億美元，支撐亞洲科技供應鏈中長期發展。短期若因市場情

緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔，較可能反映估值與持倉修正，而非AI需求基本面反轉。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化，
且全球總體經濟及地緣政治風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。

油價在美國中期選舉前，或將維持在高檔
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.14
伊朗戰爭期間卡達油氣收入暴跌
卡達政府的石油和天然氣收入 美伊衝突
• 美伊衝突短期內仍難見明確解方，彭博預估雙方後續可能採取的行動，主要可分為以下四種情境：
(1)伊朗承受經濟封鎖：伊朗承受美國經濟制裁與施壓，避免進一步重大軍事升級。在美方護航行動支持下，商船仍可維持通行
荷姆茲海峽，國際原油供應未出現明顯中斷，油價預估在每桶80美元區間波動。
(2)伊朗將攻擊範圍限於海灣地區油輪與美軍：伊朗主要鎖定海灣地區能源運輸船隻及美軍目標，美方則採取有限度軍事回應，

衝突維持在一定範圍內，油價預估在每桶90美元區間波動。
(3)伊朗擴大攻擊範圍：攻擊目標不再局限於海灣地區船隻及美軍基地，海灣能源基礎設施以及紅海等重要航運咽喉，均可能成
為目標。局勢升級期間，油價可能維持在每桶100美元以上，待衝突緩和後回落至70至80美元區間，此情境發生的可能性最高。
(4)美伊雙方全面擴大開戰：伊朗進一步擴大攻擊目標，導致美國軍事資產及區域能源基礎設施遭受重大破壞，甚至不排除以色
列重新加入戰局，油價可能升至每桶120美元。

全球債市拋售勢頭不墜，AI融資競逐資金推升美債殖利率
• 長端殖利率走高，財政與通膨壓力仍是主因 ：近期美國長
天期公債殖利率持續走高，其中10年期美債殖利率更突破
5%，創2007年以來高點；除能源價格上升推升通膨疑慮
外，美國財政赤字、政府債務及持續性的發債需求 ，也提
高市場對長天期公債供給與期限溢酬的要求 ，反映長端利
率壓力仍未完全紓解。
• AI企業擴大融資，增加公司債供給與資金競逐： AI資料中
心、晶片與電力基礎建設的資本支出快速增加 ，帶動大型
科技企業提高債券融資規模；截至 6月，華爾街市場策略
研究機構Yardeni Research在追蹤外國私人投資人過去一
年買進美國公司債約3,900億美元，高於美國公債約3,290
億美元，顯示資金配置確有轉向公司債的現象 ，不過市場

研究亦指出，AI企業發債對美債殖利率的直接推升效果相
對有限，應可視為推動長天期利率上行的眾多因素之一。
• 後市觀察：若財政融資與AI基礎建設支出持續維持高檔 ，
長天期公債仍面臨較大的供給與資金配置壓力；但 AI投資
仍具中長期成長動能 ，配置上可關注資料中心 、半導體及
電力基礎建設供應鏈 ，固定收益則以短中天期美債及高品
質投資級公司債為主，降低長天期利率波動的影響。

新興市場本幣債券擁高票息提供防禦力
• 高票息提供防禦力：對債券投資人而言，票息就像安全墊。當市場利率上升、債券價格下跌時，持續收到的票息可以部分甚
至完全抵銷價格損失。新興市場本幣政府債券今年的表現，比市場原先預期更具韌性。目前新興市場本幣債券的持有收益約
為4.54%，已經足以對利率風險形成相當程度的保護 ，即使今年存續期間對多數市場造成負面影響 ，票息收益仍讓整體報酬
維持正數。這也是為什麼新興市場本幣債券在美伊衝突爆發後一度重挫超過 6%，但之後仍能逐步反彈 。哥倫比亞、巴西、
匈牙利及墨西哥的債券表現尤其突出。
• 市場已反映相當程度的升息風險：問題在於未來幾個月投資環境可能比上半年更加困難，能源價格上漲推升通膨，也迫使新
興市場央行維持較為鷹派的貨幣政策 。市場目前甚至預期 ，19個主要新興市場中 ，有18個市場的債券殖利率可能在年底前

上升。殖利率上升意味著債券價格承壓，因此投資人不能只看表面的高票息，必須同時評估未來利率變化可能造成資本損失。
新興市場本幣債券各因子對報酬率貢獻
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.15
新興市場本幣債券票息的貢獻度

新興市場本幣債券目前最大風險在於利率與匯率
• 存續期間仍是今年最主要的拖累因素：一般而言，債券的存續期間越高，當殖利率上升時，債券價格下跌的幅度就可能越大。
今年19個符合基準指數條件的新興市場中 ，有14個市場的總報酬受到存續期間拖累 ，其中韓國、馬來西亞、印尼、泰國及
菲律賓受到的負面影響最為明顯。更值得注意的是，新興市場債券的利率表現與美國公債高度相關，因此，投資新興市場債
券，不能只觀察當地央行，也必須密切留意美國聯準會利率政策及美國公債殖利率走勢。
• 匯率可能成為下一個重要變數：新興市場本幣債券除了債券價格本身，還存在另一項重要風險：匯率。今年即期匯率變動反
而對新興市場本幣債券帶來正面貢獻 ，約增加57個基點。但如果能源價格持續上漲並形成滯脹環境 ，順周期貨幣可能面臨

更大的貶值壓力 。目前市場預期年底前新興市場貨幣整體仍偏弱 ，19種新興市場貨幣中 ，有11種預計兌美元貶值 。其中土
耳其、哥倫比亞及巴西貨幣的潛在跌幅相對較大。這代表即使債券本身提供不錯的票息，如果當地貨幣大幅貶值，換算成美
元或其他投資人本國貨幣後，仍可能侵蝕甚至吞噬債券收益。
• 小結與投資建議：目前新興市場本幣債券比較像是高票息換取較高波動的投資，而不是單純追求資本利得的交易。在殖利率
已處於相對高檔、但利率與匯率風險仍未完全解除的情況下，採取分散市場、控制部位、逢回布局的策略會比追高更為合適。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.15

• 多重資產配置可有效風險管理：美伊衝突持續及聯準會利率政策，使今年金融市場震盪加劇，加上第四季美國期中選舉到來，
增加市場更多不確定性，不過從歷史經驗來看，地緣政治事件對股市的影響多屬短期現象，市場在初期情緒性下跌後，通常會
逐步回歸基本面，相較於預測事件發展，投資人更應透過多元資產配置分散風險，以提升投資組合的穩定性與長期抗震能力。
• 透過多重資產廣納收益來源：在利率維持相對高檔的環境下，投資人更應重視收益來源的多元化，從下圖可見，相較於過去幾
年，掩護性買權、非投資等級債，及投資等級債等資產的收益率均顯著提升，提供投資組合更具吸引力的現金流來源，其中掩
護性買權收益率已超過10%，非投資等級債，及投資等級債亦分別達7%及5%以上水準，透過結合不同收益型資產，不僅有助

提升整體收益潛力，更能降低對單一市場或利率變動的依賴，在市場波動加劇時提高投資組合的穩定度與韌性，兼顧收益與長
期成長目標。

7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。
警 語

Document 062: 2026年9月进出口数据点评：进出口同比增长延续高增且远超预期

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月进出口数据点评：进出口同比增长延续高增且远超预期.pdf
Report source: 开源证券
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

事件点评 
请务必参阅正文后面的信息披露和法律声明 1 / 10

2026 年 10 月 16 日

《首次“没有全面降息”的稳增长 —
2022 年以来的“降息周期”或结束》
-2026.10.2 
《工业企业利润增速持续放缓，高基
数拖累当月增速—2026 年 8 月工业企
业利润点评》-2026.9.29 
《封顶之后， 回款之前：8·28 新政下
的 地 产 债 信 用 重 构— 固 收 专 题 》
-2026.9.29  
进出口同比增长延续高增且远超预期 
——2026 年 9 月进出口数据点评

陈曦（分析师） 王帅中（联系人）  
chenxi2@kysec.cn 
证书编号：S0790521100002 
wangshuaizhong@kysec.cn 
证书编号：S0790125070016

事件：海关总署公布 2026 年 9 月进出口商品统计快讯，按美元计价，我国进口
同比 +31.2%（前值为 +28.3%，下同 ） ，环比+11.1%(-1.1%)；出口同比 +36.3%
（+24.9%） ， 环比+11.4%（+0.9%） ； 贸易顺差同比+49.7%（+17.6%） ， 环比+12.1%
（+5.9%），进出口环比均大幅抬升。前三季度出口累计同比增长 21.3%， 进口累
计同比增长 27.5%。 
⚫ 9 月进出口数据关注点 
9 月进出口同比均延续高增，保持增长且均达 30%以上，同比增长均远超市场
预期，进出口边际上均提速。 出口同比 +36.3%（+24.9%） ，进口同比+31.2%
（+28.3%） ，进出口金额均持续处于历史高位，进口同比2026 年以来均保持两
位数增长。Wind 统计机构预测当月出口同比中位数为+25.3%， 平均数为+25.2%；
进口同比中位数为+24.5%，平均数为 23.4%，9 月进出口同比均远超市场预期，
进出口 2026 年以来始终保持高景气度，一方面是由于全球 AI 投资扩张，AI 链
上商品进出口高增拉动， 另一方面国内“内卷”与科技进步的共同推动下， 中国商
品的高性价比推动中国出口持续超预期增长。9 月超预期增长一方面与 7、8 两
月极端天气频发下订单积压，9 月以来订单积累逐步释放有关，另一方面也与 9
月全球经济保持高景气有关，9 月摩根大通全球制造业 PMI 升至 53.0，高于 8
月的 52.3，保持扩张区间。 
AI 投资扩张仍是进出口高涨的最重要因素，价格上涨在其中起到的作用也不容
忽视。AI 链上产品出口高增拉动出口增长， 集成电路出口持续加速，9 月出口同
比增长 136.8%。农产品、 化工医药、劳动密集型商品等非AI 链上商品出口同比
均保持增长，分别增长 10.8%、60.1%和 22.8%，或主要源于 2025 年同期基数较
低， 且价格上涨起到的作用不容忽视，9 月 PPI 同比上涨 5.0%， 国际油价中枢持
续攀升，价格上涨进一步推动出口金额高增，但同比增长也一定程度上反应非
AI 链条上商品出口的改善。进口同比增速重回 30%以上，AI 产业链发展仍是最
大助力。自动数据处理设备及其零部件 8 月进口同比增长 297.0%，集成电路同
比增长 75.2%。9 月美伊局势反复， 油价中枢再度攀升， 对能源进口仍有所抑制，
成品油 9 月进口同比下降 26.8%。AI 产业趋势带动相关行业的发展，已成为进
出口增长中的重要驱动力。 
从出口的产品结构来看，出口产品保持分化态势，程度上有所收窄。AI 相关产
业链发展推动集成电路、 高新技术产品出口增速高增， 带动整体增速提升， 劳动
密集型商品出口持续修复。9 月集成电路当月出口同比增长 136.8%， 高新技术产
品同比增长 63.9%， 均保持高速增长。 劳动密集型商品出口边际上持续改善修复，
4 月劳动密集型商品出口同比减少 4.0%，5 月同比减少 2.2%，6 月同比增长 5.2%，
7 月同比增长 6.6%，8 月同比增长 9.2%，9 月同比增长 22.8%，9 月环比增长 5.0%，
2025 年基数的下降对修复带来一定的支撑。我国货物贸易不断提质升级，出口
由劳动密集型产品向高质、高技术产品转变。 
从出口国家来看，9 月我国对主要国家与地区的进出口基本均保持同比增长， 出
口多元化是我国出口增速持续增长的动能所在。9 月对美国进出口同比均保持高
增，一方面与 2025 年同期基数较低有关，也与科技公司资本开支增长下对 AI
链上产品需求高增相关。从港口高频数据来看，9 月重点港口完成集装箱吞吐月
度周均值同比增长 10.4%，较前值提升 11.4pct，高频数据与实际出口情况相符，
一定程度上已提前指明本次出口超预期增长。需要注意的是，高频港口数据 10
月初有所回落， 但依旧保持高位， 全球经济保持高景气度的背景下， 四季度出口
增速整体或仍具韧性。 
⚫ 债市观点：在债市“资产荒”充分演绎后，应警惕债市调整风险 
2026 年上半年“资产荒”持续演绎，主要有三大因素驱动，资金利率持续下行、
非银久期处于低位、基本面数据走弱支撑债市走强。 
目前三大利多已经出现边际变化，警惕债市调整风险。 
资金面层面，4 月后资金利率持续回升，6 月以来 DR001 中枢稳定在 1.4%左右，
回归 2025 年下半年水平，市场宽松预期证伪； 
拥挤度层面，当前公募等非银机构债券久期已达近年高位， 存在抱团瓦解、 估值
回调风险； 
基本面层面，工业数据回暖，消费同比数据也或将随高基数效应消退逐步修复，
基本面由债市利多转为利空。此外， 市场主流的“资产荒支撑债牛”逻辑并不成立：
贷款疲软是长期的新常态，且本轮债市上涨， 由非银抱团买入驱动， 并非银行缺
资产导致的配置行为。 
整体来看，在债市“资产荒”已经充分演绎之后，特别是如果降息预期发酵/或者
落地，应警惕债市调整风险。 
⚫ 风险提示：政策变化超预期；经济变化超预期。

特别声明
《证券期货投资者适当性管理办法》 、 《证券经营机构投资者适当性管理实施指引（试行） 》已于2017年7月1日起正
式实施。根据上述规定，开源证券评定此研报的风险等级为R3（中风险） ，因此通过公共平台推送的研报其适用的
投资者类别仅限定为专业投资者及风险承受能力为C3、C4、C5的普通投资者。若您并非专业投资者及风险承受能
力为C3、C4、C5的普通投资者，请取消阅读，请勿收藏、接收或使用本研报中的任何信息。
因此受限于访问权限的设置，若给您造成不便，烦请见谅！感谢您给予的理解与配合。
分析师承诺
本研究报告的署名人员具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格或相当的专业胜任能力， 以勤勉的职业态
度，独立、客观地出具本报告，并对内容和观点负责。本报告清晰准确地反映了署名人员的研究观点，所包含的分
析基于各种假设， 不同假设可能导致分析结果出现重大不同。 本报告采用的各种估值方法及模型均有其局限性， 估
值结果不保证所涉及证券能够在该价格交易。 本报告署名人员保证他们报酬的任何一部分不曾与， 不与， 也将不会

与本报告中具体的推荐意见或观点有直接或间接的联系。
股票投资评级说明
评级 说明
证券评级
买入（Buy） 预计相对强于市场表现 20%以上；
增持（outperform） 预计相对强于市场表现 5%～20%；
中性（Neutral） 预计相对市场表现在－5%～＋5%之间波动；
减持（underperform） 预计相对弱于市场表现 5%以下。
行业评级
看好（overweight） 预计行业超越整体市场表现；
中性（Neutral） 预计行业与整体市场表现基本持平；
看淡（underperform） 预计行业弱于整体市场表现。
备注：评级标准为以报告日后的 6~12 个月内，证券相对于市场基准指数的涨跌幅表现，其中A 股基准指数为沪
深 300 指数、港股基准指数为恒生指数、新三板基准指数为三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针
对做市转让标的） 、 美股基准指数为标普500 或纳斯达克综合指数。我们在此提醒您， 不同证券研究机构采用不同
的评级术语及评级标准。我们采用的是相对评级体系，表示投资的相对比重建议；投资者买入或者卖出证券的决

定取决于个人的实际情况，比如当前的持仓结构以及其他需要考虑的因素。投资者应阅读整篇报告，以获取比较
完整的观点与信息，不应仅仅依靠投资评级来推断结论。
分析、估值方法的局限性说明
本报告所包含的分析基于各种假设， 不同假设可能导致分析结果出现重大不同。 本报告采用的各种估值方法及模型
均有其局限性，估值结果不保证所涉及证券能够在该价格交易。

### Page 1

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
Hermes Testing Solutions
Shifting-left, scaling-up: dual catalysts in chip
probing and CPO
Jack-of-all-trades for bespoke testing solutions in the semiconductor industry
We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 PT. HTSI is
uniquely positioned to benefit from two key semiconductor testing trends: shift-left
testing and the emerging CPO testing market. Leveraging its long-standing relationship

with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across electrical test engineering,
customised test equipment, thermal control modules, probe cards, and CPO testing
solutions. We forecast revenue growth of 127%/152%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, driven
by increasing adoption of thermal control modules for wafer-level test and initial
deployment of CPO insertion 2 and insertion 3 test equipment through ficonTEC.
Additional 2028E growth drivers include reflow tools, laser clean solutions, and MEMS

probe cards. The stock trades at 27/15x 2027/28E PE, versus ~35x NTM PE for handler-
equipment peers, offering re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified,
and its CPO presence expands. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Shift-left beneficiary with exceptional thermal control modules
Thermal control modules should be HTSI's largest growth driver over the next several
years. We expect increasing chip power consumption, rising package complexity, and

tighter probe pitches to drive adoption of advanced thermal control solutions used
alongside leading-edge probers, in additional to more wafer level insertions and longer
test time. We estimate HTSI will ship approximately 3,000 thermal-control modules in
2027E, up from around 1,200 units in 2026E, sales contribution rise from ~30% in
Q226 to >50% of sales by 2027E. The launch of its second-generation platform in
2027E, which carries 25-50% higher ASPs, should further improve profitability. We

forecast GM expansion from 55.5% in 2026E to 58.3%/60.0% in 2027E/28E.
Unique position to capture CPO upside
Through its partnership with German optical-automation leader ficonTEC, HTSI is
positioned to participate in the initial ramp of CPO testing equipment. HTSI supports
local manufacturing, installation, automation and after-sales service, making it a key
enabler of volume production. We estimate HTSI captures approximately 30% of
equipment value across the 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems we forecast for

delivery in 2027E, with further potential upside in 2028E as adoption expands.
Valuation: initiate coverage with a Buy rating and price target NT$8,880
We forecast EPS of NT$29.1 in 2026E (including a one-off employee share purchase
programme impact), rising to NT$153.3/286.5 in 2027E/28E. Our NT$8,880 price target
is based on 31x average 2028E PE, versus handler-equipment peers forward PE at
roughly 35x, reflecting HTSI's unique exposure to both shift-left testing, CPO, and our

estimate of 32% LT earnings CAGR. We initiate with a Buy rating.

Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will HTSI be able to keep its moat within chip probing thermal control modules?
Yes. We expect HTSI's competitive position to strengthen as chip power consumption, package
complexity, and probe density continue to increase. The company is developing next-generation
thermal control solutions, including liquid cooling technologies, while also expanding into specialized

chuck solutions designed to address wafer warpage. In addition, HTSI's close collaboration with
TSMC, TEL, Advantest, and other OSATs should reinforce its position within advanced wafer-level
testing.
Q: Will insertion 2 of CPO testing process be skipped?
No. We believe concerns surrounding insertion 2 adoption are overdone. While some industry
participants favor bypassing insertion 2 due to throughput and ROI considerations, doing so increases
the risk of defective optical engines being identified only after the components are delivered to

OSATs, creating yield accountability challenges and higher packaging losses. Based on our industry
studies, we expect insertion 2 testing to remain an important part of the CPO workflow over at least
the next two years as the industry focuses on improving PIC yields and overall testing efficiency.
We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 price target. HTSI
offers unique exposure to two of the semiconductor testing industry's most attractive growth drivers:

1) shift-left testing and 2) CPO testing. The industry's shift-left testing strategy will have more test
insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in addition to longer CP time. Leveraging its
longstanding relationship with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across thermal control
modules, electrical-test engineering services, customized equipment, and emerging CPO testing
solutions. We believe there could be re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified, and

its CPO presence expands.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI currently trades at just 15x of our 2028E EPS versus

handler-equipment peers forward PE at roughly 35x. We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO
testing equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on
our industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E,
this should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands. Our

reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR
vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for
re-rating.

Hermes Testing Solutions UBS Research
Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 3S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.

We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

We see HTSI's sales for the next few years to mainly be driven by 1) thermal control
module shipment and 2) CPO equipment shipment. In the past, thermal control

modules weren't necessary for chip probing, due to less TDP for the chips. However, as
new iterations of AI accelerators now got substantial power draw, this unlocks a brand
new TAM for HTSI to enter with their thermal control modules to pair with probers, in
order to prevent probe card damages.
 Robust thermal control module shipments.  We estimate HTSI to ship

future years, bringing their 2026-31E semiconductor equipment sales CAGR to

48%.

 Thermal control module ASP expansion.  Gen 2 thermal control modules will

Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe HTSI is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers and
capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see HTSI is uniquely positioned as
the main beneficiary of two of the strongest growth drivers within semiconductor
testing: testing shift-left and CPO.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing

### Page 5

The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging.
Figure 4: CP is set to grow substantially with shift-left, each packaging step set to be MORE costly than the prior step

RDL / RWAT testing Wafer-level CP (WLCP)
CoWChiplet (if use SoIC) CoW
CP (Die A)
CP (Die B)
Assembly Test (AST)
oS
Source: UBS
The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while
defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed

testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million
(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall

integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of
wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level

chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

Hermes Testing (HTSI) is a key beneficiary of this trend. Leveraging its long-standing
relationship with TSMC and expertise in semiconductor test solutions, HTSI supplies

advanced thermal control modules used alongside leading-edge probers (made by
Tokyo Electron), particularly for AI applications. These thermal control modules are
required for high TDP semiconductor wafer probe in order to maintain the temperature

### Page 6

of the chuck to avoid needle burns or probe card damages. We estimate HTSI will ship
approximately 3,000 thermal-management modules in 2027E, up from 1,200 in 2026E,
supporting sales growth of 127%/152% in 2026E/27E. HTSI is also the partner for
prober installation, prober maintenance and services. In addition, HTSI provides high-
end chuck processing services, an area that should benefit from increasing package sizes
and higher wafer warpage associated with advanced packaging.

Beyond thermal control modules, HTSI also supplies consumables and customised
solutions for wafer-level testing, including probe card cleaning sheets and laser-based
probe head cleaning systems. As probing intensity increases under the shift-left testing
model, demand for cleaning and maintenance solutions should rise alongside probe
card utilisation. Currently, cleaning sheets is around 5% of sales, but we believe it could
gain traction in 2027/28E and beyond. We see additional upside from future modular

laser cleaning systems that could be integrated directly with probers, further expanding
HTSI's content opportunity within advanced wafer-level testing.
CPO testing fuelling future growth for semiconductor testing
As interconnect bottlenecks increasingly shift from the memory wall to the copper wall,
we believe co-packaged optics (CPO) adoption is inevitable over the longer term. Testing
remains one of the key bottlenecks to CPO deployment, as optical validation differs

fundamentally from the electrical testing processes that have dominated semiconductor
manufacturing for decades. As a result, CPO is creating a new testing opportunity across
the semiconductor equipment and consumables ecosystem. We have written
extensively on CPO and opportunities that it brings to the semiconductor supply chain,
see our CPO deep dive , our thoughts on CPO timing , 2026 SiPhIA conference
takeaways, and SEMICON 2026 takeaways.
Various test equipment suppliers are scrambling to come up with ideal testing

equipment across the four insertions of CPO testing, with solutions to ensure both
optical and electrical signal integrity of the CPO solutions. The four insertions are:
 Insertion 1: Electronic IC (EIC)/Photonic IC (PIC) test.  Single-side wafer test
that has dedicated probe cards to test the fabricated EIC/PIC wafers.
 Insertion 2: Optical/Electrical test. Double sided wafer test, testing the hybrid
bonded (e.g. via TSMC's COUPE) EIC and PIC wafers by an opto-electrical test that

verifies the electrical signals on the top and optical signal on the bottom of the
hybrid bonded wafers.
 Insertion 3: Optical engine (OE) test.  The OE tests are done to ensure the
quality of the OEs and with FAU (Fiber Array Unit) attached, to ensure the signal
integrity of the OEs. The testing equipment needs to be able to handle 224G per
channel data rates, requiring high unit per hour (UPH) throughput, meticulous dirt
mitigation, and maintaining a near 100% contact rating on high-speed sockets.

 Insertion 4: ASIC final test.  This final test step is established post the OEs are
packaged together with the ASIC. The complete ASIC package (now with CPO) is
now tested for opto-electrical functionalities.
As it stands, insertion 2 and insertion 3 are the two areas where there are the most
debates in the industry. Insertion 3 suppliers such as Chroma has noted the trend of
“shift-to-middle” paradigm centered on insertion 3. In high-density scale-out switches

deploying up to 32 optical engines (e.g. Nvidia’s Spectrum-X architectures), any OE
defect results in package scrap at the CoWoS level, making 100% known good die
(KGD) validation at the singulated OE stage an absolute commercial requirement.

With its 25 years of precision test equipment expertise, ficonTEC is an early mover for
CPO test equipment and has key know-how for optical alignment, which is
quintessential for optical test. HTSI, through its partnership with ficonTEC, is set to be a
major beneficiary once CPO proliferates in 2027E-28E. As the partner of ficonTEC,
Hermes Testing is the key enabler that can help ficonTEC achieve HVM for its CPO
testing equipment. As of now, our view is that both insertion 2 and insertion 3 should be

required within the next two years, despite the difficulties surrounding double-sided
wafer probing (insertion 2), as the yield for OEs are still not high enough to avoid
defective dies. Finding out known-good OEs will be crucial before the FAU coupling
process during the latter stages of CPO packaging, to prevent impeding the final
package with multiple OEs attached to the switch ASIC (e.g. 32 OEs on Nvidia's
Spectrum-X). We estimate Hermes Testing will ship 40/70 units of insertion 2/3 testing

equipment in 2027E, with potential upside to the numbers as we view Hermes Testing
will likely assist with the ramp up for Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment in 2027E. Gen 2
insertion equipment will come with better throughput and ASP .

HTSI's unique position for new TAM of thermal control
modules and CPO test equipment
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan, as a spin-off
from Hermes-Epitek. The company provides semiconductor testing and inspection

solutions, engineering services, customized equipment, and test consumables. HTSI
maintains a global service footprint across Taiwan (Hsinchu, Tainan), Mainland China
(Suzhou), Japan (Tokyo, Kumamoto), Singapore, Malaysia, and the United States
(Arizona), enabling close support for leading semiconductor customers.
Its main business segments are structured around three pillars:
 Semiconductor equipment and customized products : HTSI utilises a dual-

track strategy of independent R&D and also equipment agency sales. They offers

### Page 9

highly customized solutions designed to address specific client pain points and
bottlenecks. Key products include: thermal control modules that pair with a 1:1
ratio with wafer-level test probers, electron beam inspection equipment, white
light interference optical inspection systems, laser probe cleaning machines, and
the SYMPHONY fluxless reflow system.
 Testing equipment engineering services:  The company operates the largest
engineering service team in Taiwan, consisting of over 340 technical professionals.

The engineering service team provides extensive equipment life-cycle support,
including installation, relocations, modification, testing, routine maintenance, and
repair services. CPO related equipment sales/services will be recognized within this
segment, as CPO are not direct HTSI products, but rather sales recognition via its
partnership with ficonTEC.
 Probe card design, manufacturing, and cleaning materials:  HTSI delivers a
comprehensive product portfolio that supports the entire probe card life-cycle. For

probe cards, they supply membrane, cantilever, and vertical probe cards. As they
are small in scale, HTSI targets niche probe card applications such as RF, 5G/6G,
millimeter wave, LCD driver, low end logics, TIA (Transimpedance Amplifier), etc. In
addition to the three existing probe technologies, HTSI also collaborates with MJC
in Japan for MEMS probe development, targeting sales in 2028E for high-end, AI/
HPC related applications. Cleaning materials is another important business within

this segment, as HTSI source and process sand paper or other materials to offer
cleaning sheets to clean the probe cards, removing oxidations, unwanted particles
and substances that accumulated on the probe cards during the testing process.
The sales resiliency in the coming years mainly stems from its robust relationship with key
industry players, especially as an engineering and service provider with TSMC for testing
solutions. This sets up a solid foundation for its customized equipment business, as they

know the specific testing bottleneck to tackle. For instance, as Hermes-Epitek
manufacturers the probers for Tokyo Electron (TEL) and HTSI helps with installation,
services, and maintenance of the probers, they understand the thermal bottlenecks that
the wafer-level test industry has been facing and can effectively come up with module
solutions to solve this.
Margins set to expand with value-added products and services
The company has demonstrated strong execution in advanced wafer-level testing, with

H126 revenue of NT$2.2bn already exceeding 90% of FY25 revenue (NT$2.4bn).
Profitability has also improved significantly, with GM increasing from 42.1% in 2025 to
54.9% in H126.
With share of value-added products and services set to rise in 2027E in sales mix, HTSI is
set to see GM and operating leverage expansion. As a niche engineering service provider
that has robust ties to leading foundries and OSATs, we believe HTSI has substantial
pricing power and estimate HTSI' GM for thermal control modules to be around 65%,

with GM for CPO equipment of around 60% as HTSI provides an engineering-service-
like business for it. We believe these factors will help the company to reach a 60% long-
term GM range.
OPM is also set to expand with customized equipment sales rise in total sales mix. If we
exclude the one-time expense to be recognized during Q326 for employee stock
benefits (we estimate around NT$1bn), YoY opex growth will be around 99% for
2027E, with downside risk if the company conducts tight cost control measures. We

forecast the company's OPM will expand from 19.5% in 2026E, to 44.3%/47.0% in
2027/28E.

Source: LSEG, UBS
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI still trades at just 15x 2028E P/E. We
believe market expectations for 2027E CPO testing equipment shipments remain
conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our industry analysis, we
estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this should
be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3
shipment due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO

adoption expands. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM
level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for re-rating.
We believe the market is not fully reflecting three key growth drivers:
1) Shift-left testing is still underappreciated.
As advanced packaging costs rise and AI accelerators become increasingly complex,
more thorough testing is done at the wafer-level. This should drive sustained demand

for HTSI's thermal control modules, probe card consumables, and other wafer-level
testing solutions.
2) CPO revenue potential remains underestimated.
Investor expectations for 2027 CPO equipment shipments, particularly insertion 2
testing systems, remain conservative. Based on our industry studies, we forecast HTSI
will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, with further potential
upside in 2028E as CPO adoption broadens.
3) New products provide additional optionality.

Beyond its core businesses, HTSI is expanding into higher-value solutions including mass-
reflow tools, MEMS probe cards and laser cleaning systems, creating incremental
growth opportunities that we believe are largely absent from current expectations.
We value HTSI using 31x average 2028E PE, underpinned by a 32% 2027-31E earnings
CAGR. We use 2028E PE as our view is that 2028 is the year for thermal control modules
and CPO adoption grow from the initial ramping up stage to mass adoption stage. We

believe using handlers as a peer group is justifiable for a unique company like HTSI, with
thermal control modules for chip probing sharing similar characteristics as active thermal
control used for final test, in addition to Hon Precision and Chroma having exposure to
CPO test equipment. For equipment vendors, the peer group's average NTM PE post
2022 is around 35x, and the group is currently trading at around 41x NTM PE (see
What's Priced In chart).

HTSI is trading at NT$4,190 as of 6
Oct 2026

Source: UBS estimates
Risk to the current share price is heavily skewed (2.2:1) to the upside.
UPSIDE (NT$10,000): Our upside scenario reflects stronger than expected thermal
module and CPO equipment shipment in 2027-28E, with additional upside from better
traction of probe cards and mass reflow tools, forming 157%/91% YoY sales growth in
2027/28E and even higher operating leverage with OPM of 46.3%/50.0% in 2027/28E.

This scenario implies a 34x 2028E PE, a upside driving by more equipment demand
stemming for robust shift-left test induced equipment bottleneck, alongside CPO
proliferation with scale-up CPO equipment pull-in in 2028E.
BASE (NT$8,880): Our price target of NT$8,880 is based on 31x 2028E PE. We expect an
earnings CAGR of 32% in 2027-31E. With shift-left trend for more thermal module
requirement during testing, alongside 40/70 CPO insertion 2/3 shipment. We see these

growth drivers driving YoY sales growth from a high base of 127% in 2026E to
152%/78% YoY in 2027/2028E, with OPM of 44.3%/47.0% in 2027/28E.
DOWNSIDE (NT$1,500): Our downside scenario reflects lower shipment of thermal
control modules due to less advanced prober demand with less insertion 2 and 3
equipment shipment due to bottlenecks with CPO production, alongside limited
additional growth drivers to be picked up in 2028 such as MEMS probe card and mass

reflow tools. This leads to weaker YoY sales growth of 67%/50% in 2027/28E and
operating margin of 29.3%/32.0% in 2027/28E as equipment shipment fails to scale.
This scenario implies a 14x 2028E PE, as valuation de-rate with dimmed outlook without
CPO and limited upside from thermal control module shipments.

Hermes Testing Solutions UBS Research
Company Description
Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu,
Taiwan. It focuses on providing semiconductor testing and
inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical support,
and maintenance services. Its main business segments are
customized semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and
probe cards and other materials.
Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC

applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It focuses on
providing semiconductor testing and inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical
support, and maintenance services. Its main business segments are customized
semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and probe cards and other materials.

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Hermes Testing based on PE methodology.
Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less
investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development

such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected front-end/CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in
Hermes Testing gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.
- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from

either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
Hermes Testing losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
Hermes Testing, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.
- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of

the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact Hermes
Testing’s margins for equipment shipments.

Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Document 195: 9月经济数据跟踪：经济寻底接近尾声，政策托举四季度增速

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月经济数据跟踪：经济寻底接近尾声，政策托举四季度增速.pdf
Report source: 万联证券
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 13
Status: ok

### Page 1

[Table_RightTitle] 
宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
经济寻底接近尾声，政策托举四季度增速

[Table_ReportType] 
——9 月经济数据跟踪

[Table_ReportDate] 2026 年 10 月 19 日

[T able_Summary] ⚫ 事件：2026 年Q3,GDP 不变价同比4.5%，环比1.2%。工业增加值
同比由5.2%上行至5.5%，固投累计同比持平于-7.2%，其中制造
业、基建、房地产投资分别由-2.3%、-4%、-19.9%变动为-2.1%、
-4.2%、-20.6%，社零当月同比由0.4%上行至1.1%。 
⚫ 三季度GDP 略高于市场预期，全年经济目标有望达成。2026 年三
季度GDP 不变价同比增速为4.5%，高于市场预期的4.4%。第二、
三产业对GDP 同比拉动作用边际放缓，第一产业对GDP 同比拉动
作用加强。工业产能利用率环比上行，采矿业整体小幅回升，制
造业整体小幅回落，行业分化明显。 
⚫ 工业生产季节性回升，出口拉动作用走强，高技术产业生产高位
边际放缓。工业企业出口交货值大幅拉升，出口给部分行业生产
带来较强拉动。采矿业生产持续修复，石油开采业为主要拉动，
煤炭产量跌幅收窄。制造业生产整体小幅回落，上游制造业中，
非金属矿、黑色金属冶炼行业生产上行，极端天气影响消退，施
工旺季带来需求回补，政策性金融工具加速落地，带来行业边际
改善。化工在地缘政策不确定性影响下，企业补库需求提升，行
业生产上行，有色金属冶炼及压延工业、橡胶和塑料制品业在价
格走高等影响下，生产同比回落。中游制造业表现分化，整体仍
处高位，消费品制造业整体回落。下游制造业中，汽车、运输设
备、电子设备制造同比均回落，新质生产力生产边际放缓，新旧
动能分化收敛。 
⚫ 服务业生产指数回升，生产性服务表现仍强。“双节”给服务业
带来一定提振，服务业活动预期指数持平。商务活动指数上行，
信息传输、软件和信息技术服务业与租赁和商务服务业同比小幅
上行，租赁和运输业同比均有所修复。 
⚫ 投资止跌企稳，资金投放和落地稳步推进。随着稳增长政策逐步
落地，固定资产投资逐步止跌企稳。设备购置在政策推动下持续
上行，建安工程继续下跌，新开工偏弱，投资项目不足的影响仍
存。固投资金来源来看，今年以来债券累计同比维持高位，政府
专项债用于项目建设比重明显提升，预算内资金同比跌幅有所收
窄，项目资金逐步落地。 
⚫ 地产投资持续下滑，销售依然偏弱。地产新开工、竣工跌幅加
深，施工增速持平。地产销售“金九”行情表现偏弱，二手房销
售好于新房。一线城市和部分二线城市新房价格环比上行，三线
城市价格依然偏弱，二手房价格同比多数下跌，房价依然有待企
稳。9 月 29 日居民购房贷款贴息政策公布，有望给刚需带来一定
利好，政策效果仍有待观望。房企资金来源持续收紧，销售回款
和融资环境依然承压，房企拿地偏谨慎。四季度土地供应总量收
缩的特征预期持续，结构逐步改善。 
[Table_Authors] 分析师: 于天旭 
执业证书编号： S0270522110001 
电话： 17717422697 
邮箱： yutx@wlzq.com.cn

进出口增速上行，AI、价格为主要拉动 
输入性通胀再升温，消费端刚性持续

证券研究报告|宏观报告

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 2 页 共 13 页  
⚫ 基建投资持续放缓，制造业投资反弹上行，财政资金有望给四季
度增速带来支撑。三季度以来财政资金投放有所提速，但落地进
程依然偏慢，基建下行斜率明显放缓。随着8000 亿元政策性金融
工具、政府专项债和地方政府债务结存限额在四季度投放并落
地，“六张网”建设进程有望提速，四季度基建将发挥“压舱
石”的作用。制造业投资上行部分对冲了地产、基建下行的压
力，高技术产业增速边际放缓。大规模设备更新政策、出口强韧
性，以及稳增长政策逐步落地给制造业带来拉动。 
⚫ 消费增速修复， “双节”带来提振，结构表现分化。服务消费好
转，餐饮增速上行，线上消费小幅放缓。居民实际可支配收入边
际上行，人均消费支出持平，居民消费信心窄幅震荡，内需整体
依然偏弱。可选消费结构分化，汽车为主要拖累，地产下游消费
偏弱。金银珠宝类本月跌幅收窄最为显著，通讯器材类消费持续
走高，假期出行需求提振下，石油及制品类跌幅收窄。必选消费
整体表现稳健，粮油、食品类、日用品类上行幅度较大。 
⚫ 四季度政策将发挥托底作用，关注政策落地效果。9 月我国经济
外需拉动、生产强、内需弱的格局持续，但新旧经济分化程度边
际收敛。9 月稳增长政策逐步加码，政府债发行提速，在政策扶
持下，四季度经济有望缓步回升，但仍需关注财政资金向实物工
作量的转化效率，预期全年经济节奏呈现“U 型”走势。 
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，居民购房持续低迷，我国
政策出台和落地不及预期

事件 
2026年三季度，GDP不变价同比增速4.5%， 季调环比增长1.2%， 前值0.9%。9月，工业
增加值当月同比增速上行0.3个百分点至5.5%，环比上涨0.61%，前值为0.54%。前三
季度，固定资产投资累计同比增速-7.2%，8月为-7.2%，其中，房地产开发投资累计
同比增速回落0.7个百分点至-20.6%，基建投资累计同比增速回落 0.2个百分点至-
4.2%， 制造业投资增速累计同比上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%。 社会消费品零售总额同比
增速由上月的0.4%上行至1.1%，

1    三季度经济增速略高于市场预期 
三季度GDP增速略高于市场预期，外需强劲拉动生产为主要支撑，全年经济目标有望
达成。2026年三季度GDP不变价同比增速为4.5%，市场预期为4.4%，一、二季度同比
增速分别为5%、4.3%；三季度环比增速为1.2%， 高于前值0.9%， 前三季度累计同比为
4.6%。分产业看，第一、二、三产业累计同比增速分别由上半年的3.7%、3.9%、5.2%
变动为3.7%、3.8%、5.2%，仅第二产业同比增速小幅放缓。第二、三产业对GDP同比
拉动作用边际放缓，第一产业对GDP同比拉动作用加强。二季度以来，地缘政治扰动
给全球经济、 物价带来较大风险， 我国出口保持强劲，给工业生产带来拉动， 为经济
重要支撑。 原油等大宗商品涨价压力加大， 输入性通胀被动抬升，我国工业品价格上
涨，CPI总体表现温和。 
工业产能利用率环比上行，行业分化明显。工业产能利用率小幅上行至73.1%，二季
度为73%。分行业来看，采矿业整体小幅回升，但仍低于去年同期，其中煤炭开采和
洗选环比上行幅度较大， 石油和天然气开采业小幅回落。电力、 热力、 燃气及水生产
和供应业环比回升。制造业整体小幅回落， 上游制造业中， 非金属矿物制品、 化学原
料及化学制品业产能利用率环比修复， 有色、 黑色金属冶炼及压延产能利用率同环比
下滑。中游专用设备制造产能利用率环比上行，同比高于去年同期， 通用设备制造环
比放缓，但仍处于高位，电气机械和器材制造环比上行明显。下游制造业表现分化，
计算机、 通信和其他电子设备产能利用率由78.7%上行至80.2%， 领跑其他行业； 汽车
制造业同、环比下跌幅度较大，医药制造业也有所回落。

2    工业生产回升，出口拉动作用较强 
季末月生产季节性回升， 出口拉动作用走强， 采矿业持续修复， 高技术产业生产高位
边际放缓。9月， 工业增加值当月同比增速上行0.3个百分点至5.5%，与市场预期的5.5%
一致，前三季度累计同比增速为5.3%。9月环比上涨0.61%，前值为0.54%。发电量同
比由上月的-0.8%回落至-1.2%。工业企业出口交货值当月同比由上月的11.1%上行至
17%，规模以上工业企业产销率当月同比由-0.1%下行为-0.2%。分三大门类看，9月，
采矿业同比上行2.7个百分点至1.3%，石油和天然气开采业本月同比上行幅度相对最
大，原煤产量同比跌幅收窄，天然原油产量同比上行，天然气产量同比由正转负。制
造业同比由上月的6.1%回落至6%； 高技术产业同比由上月的16.7%回落至15.3%。 电力、
燃气及水的生产和供应业同比上行0.7个百分点至5.6%。

图表3：采矿业持续修复，高技术产业高位放缓（%） 图表4：工业企业出口交货值增速攀升（亿元，%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

化工、 黑色金属行业生产上行， 下游制造业生产整体放缓， 消费品制造业整体回落。
细分行业来看， 上游制造业中，化学原料及化学制品制造业增速持续上行， 中东地缘
政治给供应链和原料价格带来较大扰动， 不确定性下企业补库需求提升， 乙烯、 化纤
产量上行。 非金属矿物制品、黑色金属冶炼及压延工业同比修复上行， 钢材、 水泥产
量同比跌幅收窄，极端天气影响消退后， 施工旺季带来需求回补， 政策性金融工具加
速落地， 叠加原材料成本上涨带来行业边际改善。 有色金属冶炼及压延工业、橡胶和
塑料制品业同比回落， 价格高位给生产带来一定抑制。中游制造业表现分化， 整体仍
处高位， 其中，通用设备、电气机械及器材制造业同比回落，专用设备制造业同比上
行。下游制造业中，汽车制造业同比回落2个百分点至6.7%，下滑幅度较大，高基数
带来一定影响；铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业同比回落1.7个百分点
至11.7%，增速持续放缓。计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业同比回落，工业机器
人产量同比下滑幅度较大，集成电路产量同比下行，微型电子计算机产量跌幅收窄。
消费品制造业中，医药制造业同比回落0.3 个百分点至 4.9% ， 农副食品加工业、食品
制造业生产增速放缓，纺织业同比小幅上行。

服务业生产指数回升， “双节”带来一定提振，商务活动也有所修复，生产性服务表
现仍强，服务业活动预期指数持平。服务业生产指数9月当月同比为4.8%， 较前值4.1%
显著上行， 中秋节和十一长假给服务业带来一定提振， 前三季度累计同比为4.7%，与
8月累计同比增速持平。服务业活动预期指数持平于8月的55.5%，当前居民消费依然
受到信心因素制约。 商务活动指数当月同比由49.3%上行至50.2%，信息传输、 软件和
信息技术服务业与租赁和商务服务业同比小幅上行，租赁和运输业同比均有所修复，
生产性服务依然相对强势。

3    投资：地产、基建延续磨底，制造业增速边际修复 
投资增速止跌企稳，资金投放和落地稳步推进，设备购置在政策推动下持续上行， 建
安工程继续下跌。2026年前三季度， 固定资产投资累计同比增速-7.2%，8月为-7.2%，
高于市场预期的-7.5%。固投季调环比增速为0.07%， 前值为-0.55%。随着稳增长政策
逐步落地， 固定资产投资逐步止跌企稳。其中， 房地产开发投资累计同比增速继续回
落0.7个百分点至-20.6%；基建累计同比增速回落0.2个百分点至-4.2%，制造业累计
同比增速上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%，民间固定资产投资回落0.3个百分点至-10.4%。
设备工器具购置同比增速由9.3%上行至9.7%，设备更新政策效果持续显现；建筑安装
工程同比由-9.8%回落为-10%，新开工偏弱，投资项目不足的影响仍存。第二产业投
资增速上行至主要支撑， 第三产业投资增速小幅回落， 第一产业下滑幅度较大。固投
资金来源来看，今年以来债券累计同比维持高位， 政府专项债用于项目建设比重明显
提升，预算内资金同比跌幅有所收窄，项目资金逐步落地。

3.1  房地产投资持续下滑，销售依然偏弱 
房地产投资增速持续下探，新开工、竣工跌幅加深。2026年9月，房地产投资累计同
比增速继续回落0.7个百分点至-20.6%，具体来看，房屋新开工面积累计同比增速回
落0.1个百分点至-24.9%，房屋施工面积累计同比持平于上月的-12.8%，竣工增速由
-23.7%回落为-25.1%。 
地产销售“金九”行情表现偏弱，二手房销售好于新房。9月，商品房销售面积累计
同比由-12.1%继续下探至-13.2%， 商品房销售额由-13%下跌为-13.9%， 房地产销售 “量”
的跌幅相对 “价” 更大。从70城房价数据来看， 一线城市新房和二手房销售价格环比
上涨， 二手房价格同比多数下跌，仅上海同比上行。 部分二线城市新房销售价格环比
上行， 三线城市新房、 二手房价格同环比持续放缓， 房价依然有待企稳。二手房交易
网签面积累计同比由10.6%小幅上行至10.7%， 二手房销售好于新房的格局持续， 但二
手房交易景气难以传导至房企回款与投资。商品房待售面积累计同比由-1.1%下跌至
-1.5%， 其中住宅累计同比由-0.5% 下行至 - 0.7% ， 地产库存持续去化。 8 月地产政策陆
续出台， 北京、 上海等核心城市购房调节进一步放松。 地产直至竣工验收具备交付条
件后方可收取购房资金和按揭贷款，优化了居民的购房环境，但从9月数据来看提振
效果相对有限。9月29日居民购房贷款贴息政策公布， 政策在10月1日期实施， 有望给

房企资金来源持续收紧， 销售回款和融资环境依然承压。房地产开发资金来源增速由
-21%回落为-21.6%。其中，个人按揭贷款跌幅小幅收窄，二手房交易活跃带来支撑，
定金及预收款增速与上月持平， 地产 “金九” 行情偏弱， 地产销售回款受限。 国内贷
款跌幅小幅修复上行， 自筹资金增速进一步下滑，地产行业信贷投放保持审慎， 房企
资金来源持续承压。 
房企拿地同比持续回落， 去库背景下， 房企依然偏谨慎。9月，100大中城市成交土地
占地面积环比季节性上行， 但同比由-16.1%继续小幅下调至-16.8%，房企拿地依然集
中在一线城市与核心城市的优质地块。房地产仍处去库进程当中，预售资金制度改革
影响将逐步显现， 房企资金周转受到约束， 叠加销售端和资金来源的限制，四季度土
地供应总量收缩的特征预期持续，结构逐步改善。

3.2  基建增速持续放缓，财政资金落地有望给行业带来托举 
基建投资持续放缓，财政资金有望给四季度增速带来支撑。 9 月， 基建累计同比增速
继续回落0.2个百分点至-4.2%， 三季度以来财政资金投放有所提速， 基建下行斜率明
显放缓， 但落地进程依然偏慢。 随着8000亿元政策性金融工具、 政府专项债和地方政
府债务结存限额在四季度投放并落地， “六张网” 建设进程有望提速， 四季度基建将

发挥“压舱石”的作用。

和公共设施管理业跌幅进一步加深， 且下滑幅度最大。交通运输、 仓储和邮政业增速
持续下探， 但下行速度边际放缓。 电力、 热力、 燃气及水的生产和供应业增速小幅修
复上行，但仍为负增，随着高基数效应减弱，增速有望边际修复。

3.3  制造业投资反弹上行，高技术产业增速放缓 
制造业投资反弹上行，高技术产业增速边际放缓，出口和政策为主要拉动。9月，制
造业累计同比增速上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%，部分对冲了地产、 基建投资下行的压力，
民间固定资产投资回落0.3个百分点至-10.4%，依然表现偏弱；高技术产业累计增速
由5.2%回落至4.9%，设备工器具购置同比增速由9.3%上行至9.7%。 大规模设备更新政
策、 出口强韧性， 以及稳增长政策逐步落地给制造业带来拉动，上游资源品、 关键材
料等自主补库需求提高，也给制造业带来支撑。四季度随着财政资金投放陆续落地，
给制造业的支撑效果有望逐步显现。

4    消费增速修复，结构表现分化 
消费增速修复上行， “双节”带来提振，服务消费好转，线上消费放缓，内需整体依
然偏弱。9月， 社会消费品零售总额同比增速由上月的0.4%上行至1.1%， 市场预期0.7%，
累计同比持平于上月的1.1%。其中， 除汽车以外的消费品零售增速由上月的2.5%上涨
为3.9%， 上行幅度大于整体； 餐饮同比由上月的1.1%上涨为2.1%； 限额以上企业零售
同比由上月的-3.7%上涨为-2.1%。实物商品网上零售累计同比由4.3%回落为3.8%，网
上商品和服务占社零比重高位小幅下滑。城镇消费同比由0.2%上行至0.8%， 农村消费
由1.6%回升至2.7%，两者差距再度走扩。 
居民实际可支配收入边际上行， 人均消费支出持平，居民消费信心依然偏弱。2026年
前三季度， 全国居民人均可支配收入名义累计同比增长5.3%， 上半年为5.2%；扣除价
格因素实际累计同比4.4%， 前值为4.2%； 居民人均消费支出累计同比持平于上半年的
3%。从数据更新至8月的消费者信心指数来看，居民消费信心和意愿均小幅上行，但
收入信心指数持续回落。居民存款缓步回落， 就业和收入压力下， 信心有待进一步修
复。 
图表18：居民存款高位缓步回落（%） 图表19：消费者信心指数上行，收入信心指数回落

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

可选消费结构分化， 汽车为主要拖累， 地产下游消费偏弱， 必选消费表现稳健。可选
消费中，金银珠宝类本月跌幅收窄最为显著，黄金价格持续回落，高基数效应走弱，
给增速带来一定拉动。地缘政治影响下， 油价持续走高，在中秋和十一出行需求提振
下， 石油及制品类跌幅收窄。 汽车消费跌幅相对最大， 消费补贴和税费优惠政策退坡
带来一定影响。 地产下游行业中， 家电增速小幅放缓， 仍保持正增， 家具、 建材类跌
幅明显收窄。消费补贴对耐用品消费的透支作用逐步显现， 居民消费信心偏弱， 汽车、
地产后周期产品消费持续承压。 通讯器材类在高基数下， 增速持续走高， 新品发布周
期，内存价格大涨拉升了手机售价。必选消费增速均修复上行，其中粮油、食品类、
日用品类上行幅度较大。

5    失业率季节性回落，结构压力仍存 
失业率季节性回落， 农民工就业压力加大。9月，城镇调查失业率由5.3%下行至5.2%，
31个大城市城镇失业率也由5.3%下行至5.2%，外来农业户籍人口失业率持平于8月的
5.0%，BCI企业招工前瞻指数边际回落。9月就业人员平均工作时间由48.2小时/周上
行至48.3小时/周。从年龄结构来看，更新至8月的失业率数据中，16-24岁的年轻劳
动力失业率持续走高，25-29岁也有所上行，大学生毕业季带来的影响持续。随着大
学生就业逐步被吸纳，9月毕业季带来的影响季节性减弱。

6    总结与展望 
四季度政策将发挥托底作用，关注政策落地效果 。9月我国经济外需拉动、生产强、
内需弱的格局持续，但新旧经济分化程度边际收敛。9月稳增长政策逐步加码，政府
债发行提速， 在政策扶持下， 四季度经济有望缓步回升， 但仍需关注财政资金向实物
工作量的转化效率，预期全年经济节奏呈现“U型”走势。

7    风险提示 
地缘政治风险发展超预期，居民购房持续低迷，我国政策出台和落地不及预期。

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

Document 242: AI数据中心泡沫将近？英伟达支持的Nscale估值接近腰斩，微软也撤了

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI数据中心泡沫将近？英伟达支持的Nscale估值接近腰斩，微软也撤了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

英国新云计算公司 Nscale 因电⼒接⼊延误遭微软撤单，且 IPO 估值被投资者要求降⾄ 160 亿
⾄ 190 亿美元，较原计划缩⽔超四成。这成为⼀个⽉内第五宗折戟的 AI 数据中⼼上市案，反
应出供电瓶颈、巨额亏损与⾼融资成本正重创 AI 基础设施泡沫。
摘要：
英伟达背书、坐拥千亿美元合同承诺的英国新云计算公司 Nscale ，正遭遇与⼀个⽉前澳⼤利亚
同⾏ Firmus 如出⼀辙的困境： IPO 估值被投资者要求接近腰斩，微软宣告撤出关键数据中⼼项
⽬。
据彭博报道， Nscale 此前寻求以约 300 亿美元估值上市，但已有⼀批投资者明确表⽰， 160 亿⾄
190 亿美元 " 更可能获得买家认可 "—— 较原定估值缩⽔逾四成。与此同时，据路透援引《电讯
报》的报道，微软已终⽌与 Nscale 位于英国埃塞克斯郡洛顿（ Loughton ）数据中⼼园区的合作
协议，原因是英国国家电⽹官员告知 Nscale ，该站点最早可能要到 2030 年代才能获得供电。微
软对此拒绝置评。

这是不到⼀个⽉内第五宗 AI 数据中⼼上市计划遭遇挫折的案例，前四宗分别涉及软银旗下 SB
Energy 、 Firmus 、菲律宾 PLDT ⼦公司 Vitro 及⿊⽯旗下 AirTrunk 。⾼盛信贷团队将这⼀系列事件
归结为 "AI 交易遭遇资⾦成本 " ，⽽信贷市场早在数周前已通过债券价格和信⽤违约互换的恶化率
先发出预警。
微软撤局：供电⽆期，锚定客⼾先⾏离场
洛顿项⽬的失败，反映出当前 AI 基础设施建设的核⼼⽭盾：合同可以⼀天之内签订，电⽹连接却
可能遥遥⽆期。
据路透报道，微软终⽌协议的直接导⽕索是国家电⽹的告知 —— 该站点直⾄ 2030 年代都⽆望接
⼊电⼒供应。 Nscale ⽅⾯回应称，与微软的 " 关系依然稳固且⻓期 " ，将此次问题定性为 " 伦敦范
围内的电⽹问题 " ，并表⽰正在 " 探索替代能源⽅案 " 。然⽽，锚定客⼾已然离场，这⼀表态难以
弥补实质性的商业损失。
微软今年已不⽌⼀次选择撤出第三⽅ AI 算⼒项⽬ —— 据彭博此前报道，今年 6 ⽉，微软曾拒绝了

⼀笔价值 30 亿美元的甲⻣⽂云计算合同。当全球现⾦储备最为充裕的超⼤规模云服务商开始对
租⽤算⼒挑剔起来，主打 " ⽐任何⼈更快上线算⼒ " 的新云计算公司的核⼼商业逻辑便受到直接挑
战。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

挪威旗舰园区的情况同样不容乐观。 Nscale 位于北极圈以北的格洛姆峡湾（ Glomfjord ）设施已
延误六个⽉，原因涵盖冷却系统泄漏、当地法规、建设延误及⽕灾事故。计划中 30 兆⽡的容
量，⽬前实际运⾏的仅有 6.4 兆⽡。
千亿合同下的真实账本： 4% 的落地率与 107 倍的估值泡沫
Nscale 的招股说明书在头部数字上颇为亮眼，但细究之下，数据显⽰出现实与承诺之间的巨⼤
鸿沟。
公司披露的合同总价值⾼达 1034 亿美元，较两年半前创⽴时的约 1 亿美元急剧扩张。然⽽实际在
运营的合同价值仅为 26 亿美元；已上线容量为 55 兆⽡（其中 48 兆⽡为租⽤托管），⽽已签署的 IT
容量承诺约为 1346 兆⽡，实际落地⽐例仅约 4% 。微软（合同上限 438 亿美元）与 Anthropic （合
同上限 446 亿美元，均来⾃尚未获得融资的西弗吉尼亚园区）合计占订单储备的约 85% ，⽽
Anthropic 合同条款允许其⽆附加义务退出，且相关融资尚未到位。
财务层⾯， Nscale 2026 年上半年营收为 1.406 亿美元，净亏损达 10.2 亿美元，即每获得 1 美元收

⼊，便亏损约 7 美元。按上半年营收年化计算，约 2.8 亿美元的年营收对应 300 亿美元估值，意味
着市销率⾼达约 107 倍。即便按投资者认可的 160 亿⾄ 190 亿美元区间定价，市销率仍在 57 ⾄ 68
倍之间。
此外，英国《⾦融时报》曾披露 Nscale 在招股材料中淡化了其与字节跳动的关系 —— 后者去年
贡献了其近四分之三的销售额。 Nscale 于 9 ⽉ 18 ⽇公开提交招股⽂件，强制等待期早已届满，但
据彭博报道，公司⽬前仍在 " 处理美国证券交易委员会的意⻅ " ，尚未启动正式路演。
连环折戟：新云计算 IPO 窗⼝接连关闭
Nscale 的困境并⾮孤例，⽽是近⼀个⽉内 AI 数据中⼼融资浪潮退潮的缩影。
这轮折戟浪潮始于 9 ⽉ 22 ⽇：软银旗下 SB Energy 原计划为全球最⼤数据中⼼募资，因投资者对
其逾 500 亿美元估值望⽽却步， IPO 被迫延期⾄今仍未启动正式营销。随后是 Firmus 先削减 IPO
价格 25% ，继⽽彻底撤回上市计划；菲律宾 PLDT ⼦公司 Vitro 将数据中⼼ REIT 上市推迟⾄ 2027

年；⿊⽯旗下 AirTrunk 即便将收益率提升⾄约 6% ，其新加坡 REIT 的 15 亿美元 IPO 同样延⾄ 2027
年。
信贷市场早已率先发出警报。， CoreWeave 的信⽤违约互换价差已飙升⾄历史纪录，隐含五年内
违约概率约为 63% ；其 2032 年⽆担保债券收益率触及 15%。 Gartner 预测，逾 80% 的新云计算公
司将在 2030 年前终⽌独⽴运营。⾼盛信贷团队在其周末报告中写道：
" 资本并未消失，只是变得更具甄别⼒ …… 市场⽇益区分那些能够⾃主融资增⻓的公司，与那些
增⻓依赖融资持续可得的公司。 "

### Page 3

⾼盛⾼产值信贷团队还指出，样本⾼收益数据中⼼项⽬融资债券的平均价格已从 6 ⽉的 101.5 美
元跌⾄ 10 ⽉的 94.7 美元， " 建设⻛险、再融资敞⼝、租⼾担保、电⼒供应及审批许可 " 正成为信贷
承销的核⼼考量 —— ⽽恰恰是电⼒供应与审批问题，直接导致了 Nscale 在洛顿失去微软这⼀锚
定客⼾。
估值重定价蔓延股市：科技板块连遭抛售
信贷市场的压⼒已充分传导⾄权益资产。 CoreWeave 上周市值蒸发约四分之⼀， Nebius 两周内
累计下跌 32% 。据⾼盛全球股票衍⽣品团队报告，超⼤规模云服务商的信⽤利差已扩张⾄ 2025
年以来最宽⽔平，⽽历史上当利差处于类似区间时，相关股票的价格⽔平较当前低约 15% 。
⾼盛技术、媒体与电信专家 Peter Callahan 指出，纳斯达克 100 指数上周录得 2025 年 4 ⽉以来最
差单周表现，跌幅约 550 个基点，半导体板块领跌约 11.5% 。费城半导体指数（ SOX ）已连续⼋
个交易⽇下跌，创 2008 年以来最⻓连跌纪录；半导体板块估值已从今年夏季约 29 倍远期市盈率

压缩⾄约 17 倍。⾼盛 AI 受益股票篓⼦较 6 ⽉⾼点相对⼤盘已回落 25% 。
下⼀步：私募市场或是唯⼀出路
就眼下⽽⾔， Nscale ⾯临两条路径。据彭博报道，相关讨论仍在进⾏， IPO 计划的细节可能发⽣
变化，尚⽆最终决定。
选项之⼀是接受估值打折，按 160 亿⾄ 190 亿美元定价上市—— 这仅略⾼于今年 3 ⽉ Series C 融资
时 146 亿美元的估值，对于今年 9 ⽉由 Dan Loeb 旗下 Third Point 领衔、英伟达、 Apollo 及 Citadel
跟投、合计注⼊ 33.6 亿美元的 Pre-IPO 投资⼈⽽⾔，这将是⼀个远⽐当初预期更为黯淡的结果。
选项之⼆是效仿 Firmus ，转向私募市场—— 但即便是 Blue Owl 也未能为⼀个 40 亿美元的数据中
⼼项⽬找到第三⽅资⾦。
更⼤的市场背景同样不容忽视。银⾏家⾯临 " 多重⻛险交织 " 的局⾯， IPO 市场正对除 Anthropic 以
外的⼏乎所有 AI 项⽬趋于冻结。⽽ Anthropic 作为 Nscale 订单储备的两⼤⽀柱之⼀，其⾃⾝上市

计划亦可能⾯临延迟。届时， Nscale 将与最⼤客⼾之⼀争夺同⼀批 AI 主题投资者的资⾦ —— 这
⼀处境颇为微妙。

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As was universally expected, the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) lifted the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6% at its
September 2026 meeting.
This is quite the turnaround from the 11 August meeting,
where an extended period on hold looked to be the base case.

At that time, the data had broken against the RBA’s more
hawkish view, and its published forecasts did not support
further hikes. But the MPB can now point to the emergence of
some of the upside risks to inflation it warned of in August.
The post-meeting statement was notable for its emphasis
on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices
are now much higher than had been assumed in the August
forecasts” – “much higher” is stronger language than one

normally sees in an RBA statement. The RBA’s liaison indicates
that firms are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-
through was characterised as partial, and additional to
domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We
read this as implying that the RBA is concerned about upside
risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction
costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data

centre boom, although in the media conference, the Governor
clarified that risks there were building rather than materialising.
The broader background highlighted in the Governor’s opening
statement to the media conference, is of demand outstripping
supply. The statement evidenced this by noting the stronger
July CPI and June quarter GDP results than expected.
The accompanying statement was notable for the absence of
language about the labour market being a little tight, despite

this being the inter-meeting message from the Governor
and other staff (see, for example, the Governor’s opening
statement on 18 September) and the language in the post-
meeting media conference. Views clearly differ on this: we
note that the Treasury forecasts underlying the May budget
and the recent Intergenerational Review imply both more
spare capacity in the labour market (a lower NAIRU) and faster
growth in potential from wherever the starting point is (higher

productivity growth and trend increase in participation).
Instead, the post-meeting statement characterised the
labour market as easing “broadly as expected”, despite the
unemployment rate already rising higher than the August SMP
forecast of 4.5% for the December quarter.
RBA hikes in September, November follow-up now expected
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The decision was unanimous, suggesting that any qualms
external MPB members might have had about the RBA’s

downbeat view of supply capacity were overruled by resurgent
oil prices and the stronger than forecast CPI and GDP data.
With upside risks being seen to have emerged, the post-meeting
statement language included that the Board “will continue to
do what it considers necessary to bring inflation sustainably
back to target, including increasing the cash rate target further if
needed”, a small but meaningful change from the “if upside risks
materialise” language from the previous meeting, and back to

the tone of the language at the June meeting.
The bar for a follow-up hike in November is low. Indeed,
judging by the post-meeting rhetoric, a November hike is now
the base case, absent a lasting resolution of the Middle East
conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly
lowers the outlook for energy-related costs in Australia. The
bar for hikes beyond that is much higher, given the cumulative
rise in interest rates, and noting that the labour market is

easing and the housing market will likely weaken further.
The August inflation data released the day after the meeting
was in line with our expectations. We therefore continue to
expect Q3 trimmed mean inflation around 0.9%qtr, too high for
the RBA’s comfort, and upside risks for Q4. Likewise, there are
no implications for our rates view from this week’s inflation data.
Forecast changes
• We have updated our financial forecasts to reflect the change

in our rate view.
• Our labour market forecasts are unchanged on the first
decimal place, but there is some modest upside risk to our
unemployment forecast.
• Our updated GDP profile can be found on page 9.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
led the correction to date, the fact that the other capitals
are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest
rates collide with the tax policy changes announced in May’s

budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced
by the continued pull-back in investor credit growth . Westpac
anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For
more, see our latest Housing Pulse.
Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength
in household demand and higher energy prices. Headline PCE
inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased

0.2%mth and 3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the
share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin
Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%, suggesting some easing in
broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household
spending remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent
to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal income
growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling

households opted to draw down savings.
The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the
headline suggested. While the index edged down 0.1pt to
54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive
months. There were some signs of capacity constraints
emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs
up 4.6pts to 56.4, suggesting that firms struggled to keep
pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs

increased. Firms responded by lifting employment, 1.5pts to
52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed
acceleration in cost pressures, with the prices paid index
jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and
ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every major industry
reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the

clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in this week’s busy
Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes,
citing strong inflation, diminished supply capacity and policy
that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing
to progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time
to assess upcoming data. The one thing both agree on is that
inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and
extent of further rate hikes. Our view remains that one further

hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary
Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the cash rate by 25bps
to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given
Governor Bullock had already communicated that upside risks
to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established
by the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.
Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks.

The Middle East conflict was emphasised as the risk that had
“materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude
oil and refined product prices over recent months. The RBA’s
liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of costs
and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic
capacity pressures and the data centre/AI investment boom were
also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.
In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis

explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in November is
low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided
there is no sudden resolution of the Middle East conflict
beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the
outlook for energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying
(trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr), which was
slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s
forecast. As usual, the monthly detail was mixed, but the key

drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-
related components, particularly rents and new dwelling
purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with the
unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel
prices. We have maintained our forecast for a 0.9% lift in
trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building
for Q4, a follow-up rate hike in November appears most likely.
Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending

surprised with a flat reading in August. Services spend fell back
following recent strength that was driven by major sporting
events, but this was offset by an increase in goods spend,
particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending
pulse is shading a touch softer. However, another solid
outcome for business investment could once again be on the
cards for Q3. August’s goods trade data showcased another

significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our
modelling suggests that the local economic impact of such
investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s
latest update revealed that dwelling prices fell another 1.1%
nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics

Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted
support measures aimed at stabilising growth and supporting
the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its
Pledged Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and
expanded quotas for targeted lending programs. Authorities

also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The
subsidy applies to homes with a floor area of 120m² or less
and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of
mortgage costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of
five years. The measures complement reforms announced in
August to improve developer financing conditions and support
housing completion. However, weak credit demand and
confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy

transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement
in activity, although the details were less convincing.
Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts
to 50.1, supported by stronger production and new orders.
Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in
prices, with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and
producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market conditions
remained weak, with the employment index falling a further

0.6pts and remaining below 50 for almost four years. Conditions
in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the
services PMI lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger
construction activity. Input and output prices moved higher,
selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved
to 48.5 and employment increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it
remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some

traction, but weak labour demand, subdued credit growth and
soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another
constructive signal on the economy and reinforced the case
for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved
by 2pts for large manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while
profitability indicators strengthened and capital expenditure
intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect

fixed investment to increase by 11.6% in FY2026, while large
non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%,
pointing to continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both
manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with measures
of excess production capacity becoming less negative
and indicating that demand remains sufficiently strong to
keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also

intensified, with employment conditions becoming more
negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a
progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations
remained anchored around 2%, providing further evidence
that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation
environment. Overall, the survey was consistent with an
economy operating close to capacity and supports the view
that a December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Resilient activity and simmering inflation
While bumpy and uneven, New Zealand’s economic
recovery has continued over the past few months, with
activity showing resilience in the face of continued global
headwinds. Under the surface, however, inflation pressures

are bubbling away, with much of that pressure centred on
parts of the economy that aren’t sensitive to interest rates.
Economic activity getting back on firmer footing
Looking first at recent activity data, the economy has been
finding its feet again after taking a knock in the wake of the
Middle East war. That has been seen most clearly in the jobs
market, with the number of filled jobs rising by 0.9% in the
year to August. While not shooting the lights out, that is still a

noticeable improvement from the early part of the year, when
filled job numbers were falling.
Under the surface, it’s still a very mixed picture. Agriculture
remains the main engine of growth, with the strength in
commodity export earnings helping to boost incomes,
confidence and spending in regions with strong rural backbones.
That’s supported firm job growth in regions like Canterbury
(where filled job numbers are up 1.9% over the past year), Otago

(+1.5%) and Southland (+3.1%). In contrast, jobs growth remains
much more moderate in Auckland (up 0.6%) and Wellington
(up just 0.4%). That town-and-country split is likely to remain a
feature of the economic landscape for some time yet.
Looking ahead, businesses expect trading conditions to
remain resilient through the final months of the year. That
was reflected in September’s Business Outlook survey.
While the survey’s gauges of expected business activity and

general business confidence eased over the past month, they
remain at firm levels, consistent with continued moderate
growth through the latter part of the year. That resilience has
also been evident in the household sector, with consumer
confidence largely steady through September.
The resilience in household and business sentiment is notable,
as fuel prices have taken a big step higher over the past month.
The average price of 91 unleaded has risen by around 49c/litre –

albeit late in the month and likely not fully reflected in surveyed
confidence – taking it back to the levels seen during the early
stages of the Middle East conflict. Those higher fuel prices
have taken a bite out of household disposable incomes, with
spending on fuel up about 22% compared with this time last
year, even though the volume of fuel sold is down around 11%.
But even with the lift in fuel prices, we haven’t seen the same
degree of pessimism as earlier in the year, when fuel prices first

spiked. Notably, those higher fuel prices haven’t spilled over
into a broader lift in output price pressures either (at least at
this stage). Inflation expectations were steady in September’s
household and business surveys. Similarly, the number of firms
planning to raise prices fell back despite a lift in wage and cost
expectations. However, while that stability in pricing gauges is
welcome, measures of inflation pressure remain elevated.
Taken together, recent data reinforce indications that the

economy is shaking off the impacts of the Iran war.
Persistent inflation drivers and the RBNZ
Turning to inflation, the spike in oil prices earlier this year saw
annual inflation climb to 4.1% in the year to June. With local
fuel costs rising again in recent weeks, we continue to expect
inflation to remain above 3% until at least mid-2027.
However, even before the recent rise in fuel costs, inflation had
been running hot for an extended period. Excluding the rise

in food and fuel prices, inflation in the year to June was 2.9%,
and it’s been running close to or above 3% for five years now.
As we discuss in our recent report, much of the strength in
inflation over the past year has been related to administered
costs, such as local council rates, or to sectors that are less
exposed to competitive pressures, such as electricity and
insurance. Those increases have persisted for an extended period,
and we expect further sizeable increases in many of these costs.

This ongoing firmness is a key reason why we expect domestic
(non-tradables) inflation to be higher than the RBNZ expects
over the longer term. It’s also part of the reason why we
expect the RBNZ will ultimately need to raise the OCR by
more than it assumed in its September policy statement.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate rise at the RBNZ’s December
meeting and expect the OCR to peak at 4% next year.
This is a tough environment for the RBNZ. With ongoing

increases in administered prices boosting core inflation at a
time when energy prices are driving headline inflation well
above the target range, it’s hard for the RBNZ to look through
them. The RBNZ had little headroom to absorb upside inflation
surprises, even before the recent spike in oil prices.
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
But getting inflation back to target in the face of an oil price
spike and high administered prices will not be easy and involves
some tough trade-offs. Administered prices tend to be less
responsive to changes in economic conditions. As a result,
interest rate hikes are likely to have a bigger impact on parts of

the economy that are not the main drivers of inflation. Tighter
policy will also weigh on the labour market and near-term
growth, while increasing households’ debt-servicing costs.
This is an uncomfortable but necessary trade-off. Looking
through, or not responding to, these sorts of large and persistent
cost increases would still leave households facing significant
increases in overall living costs. In the long run, it would be
more damaging for households and the economy if generalised

inflation were to become embedded in price setting behaviour.
The Kiwi has lost its wings
Adding to near-term inflation risks has been the drop in the NZ
dollar. On a trade-weighted basis, the Kiwi has fallen around
4% since the start of September, and it’s begun the December
quarter around 4% below the level the RBNZ assumed in its
September policy statement.
The slide in the Kiwi is mainly due to the strength of the US
dollar, with NZD/USD down nearly 5% since the start of

September to sit just below 0.56 at the time of writing. Moves
against other currencies have been more modest. For example,
NZD/AUD is down 2% over the same period.
Technically, NZD/USD seems oversold. In the short term,
trends in the US dollar will dominate, including any response
to the US labour market report due on Friday evening. Any
turnaround in the USD would likely see NZD/USD trade back
towards the top of the 0.5580–0.5680 range that is governing

the pair for the time being.
NZD/AUD will continue to be driven by changing views on
interest rate differentials between the RBNZ and RBA. This
week, NZD/AUD corrected higher from around 0.8070 to as high
as 0.8110 as RBA cash rate expectations were repriced following
the RBA Board meeting and Australian CPI. In the short term, the
bias for NZD/AUD could be lower, given our view that the RBNZ
will pause at its late-October meeting and that the market is

under-pricing the risk of an RBA rate hike in November.
Next week’s QSBO, due on Tuesday, will be the key event
risk, with the inflation and growth gauges closely watched for
signs that might prompt the RBNZ to pause or hike. Markets
had previously priced in a high chance of a hike at the RBNZ’s
October meeting, but in recent days appear to be reassessing
that view.  Pricing for a 25bp hike in New Zealand could be
pared back towards a 50:50 call. The 0.8030–0.8150 range

is likely to contain the pair in the immediate period ahead.
Our medium-term view is for NZD/AUD to rise as the RBNZ
increases the OCR to 4% by the second half of 2027. We’ll
update our views on the NZD and other currencies in our next
Market Outlook, out Wednesday 14 October.
Fiscal update
The big uncertainty on the domestic horizon is, of course,
November’s general election, and at this stage the polling
looks tight. Neither the established left nor right bloc has a

clear majority.
Ahead of the election, the Treasury has released its Pre-election
Economic and Fiscal Update (PREFU), updating the Budget
2026 (BEFU) fiscal forecasts to reflect the latest economic and
fiscal information and a revised economic outlook. This allows
the public to assess the state of the Government’s finances. It
also provides other parties with a baseline against which to
calibrate their spending and taxation policies.
The PREFU revealed a stronger starting point for the

Government’s finances than had been expected in the Budget.
• A deficit of $8.6bn is estimated for 2025/26, down from the
Budget estimate of $11.9bn.
• Similarly, the forecast deficit for 2026/27 has been revised
down significantly to $6.8bn from $11.4bn.
• Thereafter, the OBEGALx deficit is expected to continue
declining gradually, moving to a surplus of $4bn in 2028/29.
This broadly follows the Budget profile, but is stronger
across the forecast period.

• Net core Crown debt is forecast to peak at 43.9% of GDP in
2027/28, below the 46.1% peak forecast in the BEFU. It’s then
forecast to decline to 39.5% of GDP by the end of the forecast
period in 2030/31, in line with the Government’s goal of
reducing debt below 40% of GDP over the medium term.
Given the improved outlook, NZDM announced a $4bn
reduction in the 2026/27 bond programme to $30bn and a
$15bn reduction in the forecast programme across the whole

period through to 2029/30.
The medium-term forecasts in the PREFU should be treated
with caution. The economic forecasts underpinning the
PREFU were finalised on 21 August, when both oil prices
and, especially, bond yields were materially lower than they
are today. Had the forecasts been finalised more recently, the
fiscal projections presented today would have been weaker,
at least in the near term. Indeed, the Treasury is upfront in
acknowledging that, while the risks are two-sided, the balance

is skewed to the downside.
Aside from the obvious uncertainty surrounding the economic
outlook because of unpredictable developments in the Middle
East, the approaching General Election, and the possibility of
a change in the composition of the Government, means the
assumptions underpinning the PREFU forecasts could quickly
become invalidated.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Oct 6, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment declined 5.2% to 84.4 in September,
taking the index back towards the deeply pessimistic levels seen
earlier in the year. Rising fuel prices and fears of an imminent

interest rate rise drove the move with a continued weakening in
housing markets likely adding to unease in some quarters.
Developments over the last month would have added to these
concerns with: the RBA raising the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6%
during the survey week; average pump prices pushing above
$2.36/litre nationally and no end in sight to global supply
disruptions;  and more weak updates on the housing market.
NZ: Q2 NZIER Survey of Business Opinion (index)

Oct 6, General business confidence last: 12.3
Business conditions picked up modestly in the June quarter as
concerns about the war in the Middle East eased. However,
cost and pricing gauges remained firm. The September quarter
survey will be closely watched ahead of the RBNZ’s 28
October meeting. Recent weeks have seen signs that activity
is firming. However, fuel prices have also risen sharply, and we
(along with the RBNZ) will be watching for signs of a broader

lift in inflation pressures. The timing of survey responses
within the sample period will also warrant scrutiny, given that
oil prices rose late in the quarter.
